#### TASK-1531 — [SAV-NET-ZERO-RESTORED-WRITE] 📋 **Amend `VER-§3` cl. 6(c): a run that writes and then restores a `.sav` proves net zero on the payload bytes, because sha256 + mtime cannot hold for it.** (manager) — marker `TASK-1531-SAV-NET-ZERO-RESTORED-WRITE` (boarded retroactively in the same action by `TASK-1529`, WRITE DISCIPLINE rule 6 · ⛔ do-not-re-dispatch)

A write-then-restore run owes a pre-run byte copy, the same size, identical bytes from the SaveGame class-path string to EOF (located in both files, never at a fixed offset), equal field reads, and the differing header range reported. The source is `qa/TASK-1524-verify.md` "Save hygiene" plus the orchestrator's byte diff (relayed, not re-measured by the manager): 276 bytes differ, all in offsets 1516–1952, and the payload from offset 1980 is identical. - **Why it is real, checked by the manager at source on 2026-09-27:** the identifiers sit inside `TEXT("…")` literals in the rows' strings, so the player reads them; they are not comments. `SiegeControlsHelpWidget.cpp` has "You can hold MaxMapMarks circles at once…" in `Interface.MapMarks`, and "Base walk speed is AHeroCharacter::WalkSpeed; … is GetEffectiveWalkSpeed() — the base is never mutated." (plus a `WalkSpeed` / `GetEffectiveSprintSpeed()` twin) in the hero movement rows. Located by text; the line numbers move.
- **My reading of the cause, labelled as a reading.** The `MapMarks` author's comment says "NO NUMBER IS TYPED: MaxMapMarks is NAMED". That obeys the first half of `HELP-§2`'s "NO NUMBER IS RESTATED IN PROSE IF IT CAN BE READ FROM DATA". The same bullet's remedy is "Prefer deriving; where a literal is unavoidable, cite its source file:line in a code comment beside it", and naming the constant in the prose follows neither half. QA's reading of the UE 5.8 source (a reading, not a run) points the other way for the Error-logging case: the cooker logs the compile error at Error, a non-zero error count forces exit 1, UAT throws "Cook failed.", and `C2-UAT-LOG` STOPs before this gate runs. - spec: >
    **The gap** (`TASK-1552` flag 1, from `handoffs/TASK-1445-programmer.md` §5): `A7-COOKDIRS` is `Test-Path` per folder only. `C2-UAT-LOG` matches only `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` / `BUILD FAILED`. Nothing reads a Blueprint compile error in the cook, and inside the eleven COOKDIRs an unreferenced broken Blueprint ships anyway (`PKG-§14`).
    (0) **Prove the route before writing it.** Find the latest real ship's cook logs on disk: the `$clogs` pair `C2-UAT-LOG` reads, under the run log dir `ship.ps1` builds (`$StagingDir\.ship\<run>\`). Quote two things from them: the cook's error/warning summary line (the sighting `TASK-1445` recorded: `Success - 0 error(s), 3 warning(s)`), and at least one `LogBlueprint` line of any verbosity, which proves that category reaches this log. **No real cook log on disk, or no summary line in it ⇒ STOP and report before writing anything.** A gate whose "cannot say" branch was never run against a real green log may stop every ship.
    (1) **The verdict function, lifted so a harness can run it** (the `Get-SuiteVerdict` / `TASK-1193` shape): `Get-CookBlueprintVerdict -Paths <logs>` returns a verdict and a reason, in this order. (i) Any line matching the literal needle `LogBlueprint: Error`, matched with `-Simple` (pattern and matcher must agree, per `ship.ps1`'s own `TASK-1193` comment) ⇒ `STOP` / `BLUEPRINT-ERROR`, quoting up to the first 5 hits. (ii) Otherwise, no cook summary line ⇒ `STOP` / `SUMMARY-ABSENT`: a zero is a verdict, never a pass (`SC-§114`). (iii) Otherwise `PASS`, with the summary line quoted as evidence. The summary's error count is printed, NOT gated: his option text scoped this check to Blueprint compile errors. If you think a stricter needle is right, say so in the handoff; it is not this row's call. Use `Get-LogMatches` (it returns an array for 0, 1 or N hits under StrictMode).
    (2) **The gate:** `Assert-Gate -Id 'C2-COOK-BP-ERRORS'`, placed right after `C2-UAT-LOG` on the live cook path and reading the same `$clogs`. Its evidence line prints the verdict, the reason and the quoted lines. Its remedy names the log path and `VER-§12` cl. 7g (a `BS_ERROR` Blueprint in the editor also wedges PIE). On the paths where `C2-UAT-LOG` is `Note-Skipped` (dry run, reused cook), note this gate skipped the same way, with the same reason text.
    (3) **The harness and the triple** (`SHIP-§9c` cl. 6; `TL-§6`: captured or truncated REAL logs, one triple per log-parsing gate). `Tools/Packaging/Fixtures/check_cook_bp_verdict.ps1` lifts `Get-CookBlueprintVerdict` and `Get-LogMatches` out of `ship.ps1` by AST and never retypes them (`SHIP-§0`). It asserts exactly one definition of each (`qa/TASK-1196-report.md` NIT 8) and cleans its temp file in `try/finally` (NIT 9). It runs three fixtures and asserts verdict AND reason for each:
      - green `Tools/Packaging/Fixtures/cook-bp-green.log`: a truncated capture of (0)'s real cook log that keeps its summary line ⇒ `PASS`;
      - red `Tools/Packaging/Fixtures/cook-bp-error-red.log`: a truncated capture of a REAL editor-log slice holding `BP_Basic_Movement`'s compile errors (the `TASK-1439` / `TASK-1557` sessions on PID 3108; find them by text in the `Saved/Logs/` backups) ⇒ `STOP` / `BLUEPRINT-ERROR`;
      - null `Tools/Packaging/Fixtures/empty.log`, 0 bytes (the file `TL-§6` names) ⇒ `STOP` / `SUMMARY-ABSENT`.
      Name each capture's source file, line range and sha256 in the handoff, not inside the log (a header would stop it being a real log). A fixture that cannot be captured from a real log ⇒ that side is NOT RUN, in those words.
    (4) **Run the harness** (a PowerShell run that writes only its temp file; declare it, `SC-§71a`) and paste the transcript: 3 of 3 verdicts and reasons as expected. **Then the mutation arm, seen red:** in your working copy of `ship.ps1`, change the needle (e.g. `LogBlueprint: Error` → `LogBlueprint: Errorx`), re-run, and show the red fixture failing the harness. Restore, and re-hash equal to your after-sha256. If your grant cannot run PowerShell, write "NOT RUN" in those words; the host then runs both before it commits.
    (5) **Honesty rider (`SC-§101`), in the handoff and in a comment above the function.** The red fixture is an EDITOR log. That the cooker writes the same `LogBlueprint: Error` line for a `BS_ERROR` Blueprint inside a COOKDIR is NOT measured. It cannot be measured without a broken asset in a cook folder, and no broken asset is ever committed to get a fixture (`VER-§12` cl. 7g). The fourth side is the next real `/ship`: its `C2-COOK-BP-ERRORS` line must read `PASS` with the summary quoted. Name that as owed, not done.
    (6) FORBIDDEN: any other gate's logic · a cook, a package or a `/ship` run · `Content/**` · `Source/**` · `CONVENTIONS.md` · mutating git.
    **Acceptance:** (0)'s quoted lines with their log paths · the function and gate diff (`git --no-optional-locks diff -U0`, read-only, declared) · the three fixtures with source, range and sha256 · the harness transcript 3/3 and the arm red → restored → green, or "NOT RUN" · before/after sha256 of `ship.ps1` · the (5) rider · `## Not examined / limitations`. Handoff `.claude/pipeline/handoffs/TASK-1567-programmer.md`.
    **ROUTING:** `ready-for-qa` → `TASK-1568`. No C++ compile and no runtime criterion, so no 5a and no 5b. Then the first commit host to derive after `TASK-1568` PASS (expected `TASK-1580`), which re-runs the harness at its own instant.
    **Slack:** ⚙️ Dev & QA (`C0BF0QZP3CN`, `1783116269.740549`), `⚙️ GAMEPLAY-PROGRAMMER:`, ≥1 post: the harness 3/3 (or NOT RUN) and the arm, emoji + TASK-1567.

- spec: >
    (0) **Start state:** the three files' sha256 equal `qa/TASK-1575.md`'s after-anchors. A mismatch ⇒ STOP.
    (1) **The candidates, in rank order** (`handoffs/TASK-1541-programmer.md` §8, items 1–5; item 6 is OUT): `Interface.MapMarks` (the circle cap, `USiegeMapMarkSubsystem::MaxMapMarks`) · `Cards.Discard` (the fee, `DiscardAllCost`) · `Hero.Rally` (`RallyRadius` / `RallySpeedBonus` / `RallyDuration` / `RallyCooldown`) · `Cards.StackUpgrade` (`MaxStackHeightMultiplier` / `StackHealthStep`) · `Hero.Attack` (melee reach, cone half-angle, cooldown). The owners and names are the handoff's: re-read each at source at your instant, and cite by text.
    (2) **Read the object the game actually uses, not the one that is easiest to read.** A Blueprint subclass may override a native default, so `GetDefault<ANative>()` is wrong wherever it does. For each number, name the class the game spawns or uses (the hero pawn from the game mode, each placeable building class, the subsystem), and read through the read-only inspector lane whether its defaults override the native value. **No PIE, and never load an asset in `BS_ERROR`** (`VER-§12` cl. 7g). Then choose per number: the live object when the help opens in a match, otherwise the spawned class's defaults. Say which, and why. **If a number differs across the classes a player can use** (for example the stack step per building type), do not pick one. Say "depends on the building" in words, or list the values, or leave that number out, and state which you chose.
    (3) **Format in player units, derived at runtime:** distances in metres (UU ÷ 100), bonuses as a percent, times in seconds, each with a fixed maximum of fractional digits (`FText::AsNumber` with `FNumberFormattingOptions`, spliced in by `FText::Format`). Put each conversion in a comment beside its format call, cited by text. No number is typed anywhere in player prose (`HELP-§2`). Reads go through existing public members or getters only; a number that would need a new accessor in its owner's file is listed and left out, not built.
    (4) **Tests, and each one must be able to fail:**
      (a) per derived number, a data-follow test: the rendered `Detail` contains the value formatted from the owning property, read in the test from the same object (2) chose (assert the state, `SC-§104`);
      (b) any existing "types NO number" assertion that a derived digit would now trip is narrowed to what is TYPED (the source template, before formatting), so a derived number passes and a typed digit still fails. Name each narrowed assertion;
      (c) the `::` / `()` guard stays green.
      **Mutation arms for `TASK-1578`**, named exactly (file, string, fragment): a typed digit injected into a template, which must turn the narrowed assertion red; and a data-follow arm (for example, formatting from the wrong property), which must turn test (a) red. State the new test count `N`.
    (5) **Size valve:** whole pages, in rank order. If all five cannot finish in one session, stop after the last whole page and list the rest.
    (6) FORBIDDEN: changing any tunable's value · editing any owner class's file · any file other than the three help files · `CONVENTIONS.md` · a compile · PIE · mutating git.
    **Acceptance:** a per-number table (page · property · owner, cited by text · the object read, and why · live value · conversion · the rendered sentence) · the tests, with their arms named · `N` · before/after sha256 and a `git --no-optional-locks diff -U0` against `TASK-1574`'s reviewed bytes · `## Not examined / limitations`. Handoff `.claude/pipeline/handoffs/TASK-1576-programmer.md`.
    **ROUTING:** `ready-for-qa` → `TASK-1577` → 5a `TASK-1578` → 5b `TASK-1579` → host `TASK-1580`.
